March 29, 2024 12:30 AM GMT

India Technology: IT Services | Asia Pacific

# How to be positioned ahead of 4QF24 results?

Post ACN's results, we are moderating our growth estimates and lowering sector EPS/PTs. A better macro set up (possibility of bounce-back in 2H), improving commentary from hyperscalers and investor positioning keep us neutral to positive, thereby holding up our multiples. Prefer INFY/TCS/HCLT.

Lowering growth rates, earnings and PT: Post-Accenture's results and commentary, we now incorporate slower conversion of deal wins to revenue (led by unfavorable mix of business towards short cycle work) leading to more gradual recovery in revenue growth vs our earlier forecasts. We have lowered our F25 revenue growth forecasts by 100-250bps across our coverage and moderated margins for some companies, driving 2-11% cuts to F25/26 EPS estimates.

Rebound hopes in 2H should keep multiples afloat: As of now, post-earnings cuts and fall in global IT stock prices, India IT valuations on a relative basis (TCS vs Accenture) appear to be in line with long-term historical averages, closing the arbitrage. Further, visibility into the demand outlook is low, limiting near-term triggers. However, we believe a positive macro set-up in 2024 vs 2023, improving commentary from global cloud companies, and the recent correction in India IT stocks (underperforming the Sensex over the past month) keep us neutral to positive on the sector. We believe hopes of recovery in 2H should keep multiples afloat, and while we have lowered our estimates, our PTs continue to imply higher than last 5-year avg multiples for TCS.

4QF24 sets tone for F25 outlook: We believe 4Q will be important for two reasons: a) it will mark bottoming out of the sector in terms of YoY revenue growth (we expect YoY growth trends to improve in 4Q vs 3Q and see QoQ growth of -1.3% to +1.6% for large caps with TCS leading the pack); b) it sets the tone for FY25 especially with full-year outlook. We believe INFY/HCLT will provide revenue growth outlook of 3-6% YoY and 5-7% YoY. In the context of recent corrections in stock prices and built-in pessimism, we think this should be a good starting point for investors. Within mid caps, we expect LTTS and Coforge to start the year with at least a double-digit revenue growth outlook for 25e with Cyient slightly slower at 8- 10%.

Key ideas: YTD mid-caps are underperforming (excl. LTTS) and large caps are outperforming (excl. LTIM). Within our coverage, Infosys is now our preferred name followed by TCS. We upgrade HCLT to OW albeit with limited upside vs. INFY/TCS. We believe HCLT offers better visibility on revenue growth than many peers in F25e (similar to TCS). We stay UW on Wipro/Tech M with a negative bias for LTIM (EW). Within mid caps we prefer Coforge and Cyient and stay UW on Tata Elxsi (slowing automotive vertical could be key concern) and LTTS.

# Investment Thesis: Still optimistic for 2H; staying selectively OW with higher target P/E than historical average

We maintain our neutral view on the sector with a positive bias...: We maintain our neutral view on the sector but with a positive bias given the better macro set-up in 2024 vs the last two years, which is keeping us hopeful of a rebound in revenue growth in 2H with room for a surprise once recovery starts. Our positive bias stems from:

1. Positive macro set-up, including a soft landing in the US with hopes of rate cut, US GDP growth seeing upgrades for 2024, S&P500 corporate earnings stabilizing in 2024 vs declining through 2023, and US banks seeing more constructive outlook than before (as noted by our US banks analyst).

Exhibit 5: Consensus S&P 500 EPS expectations have been holding up well
![](images/dcd01736b99db208349c6ffc5d1768ac95da6bbeccbc8b686a783da6550f97b9.jpg)
Source: Refinitiv, Morgan Stanley Research estimates

Exhibit 6: Consensus S&P 500 Banks Index EPS expectations moved up in past few months
![](images/36d023daa3a256107152cfcd416e4ed8491e593535c80e8a2eaa202717a15795.jpg)
Source: BBG estimates, Morgan Stanley Research
2. Commentary of hyperscalers pointing to stability and some potential re-acceleration in migration of new workloads

![](images/15361c37f8b64424b6501ba679fac5d6475d6d99bd325c14b17f0d5b095db16c.jpg)
Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research; E=Morgan Stanley Research estimates
Note: Hyperscalers' revenue refers to blended average of AWS, Microsoft Azure and Google Cloud.

Exhibit 8: Verbatim commentary from hyperscalers – Pointing to some recovery trends
<table><tr><td>Amazon (4Q23)</td><td>Similar to what we shared last quarter, we continue to see the diminishing impact of cost optimizations. And as these optimizations slow down, we&#x27;re seeing more companies turning their attention to newer initiatives and reaccelerating existing migrations. Customers are also excited about our approach to generative Al. Still relatively early days, but the revenues are accelerating rapidly across all three layers, and our approach to democratizing Al is resonating well with our customers....As we look forward to 2024, we anticipate CapEx to increase year-over-year primarily driven by increased infrastructure CapEx to support growth of our AWS business, including additional investments in generative Al and large language models.</td></tr><tr><td>Microsoft (2Q24)</td><td>And I think last quarter, we said, one, we are going to continue to have these cycles where people will build new workloads. They will optimize the workloads and they&#x27;ll start new workloads. So I think that, that&#x27;s what we continue to see. But that period of massive, I&#x27;ll call it, optimization only and no new workload start, that, I think, has ended at this point. So what you&#x27;re seeing is much more of that continuous cycles by customers, both whether it comes to Al or whether it comes to the traditional workloads.</td></tr><tr><td>Google (4Q23)</td><td>We&#x27;re very pleased with the momentum of GCP with an increasing contribution from Al...there are regional variations, but the cost optimizations in many parts are something we have mostly worked through...Our reported CapEx in the fourth quarter was $11 billion, driven overwhelmingly by investment in our technical infrastructure with the largest component for servers followed by data centers...In 2024, we expect investment in CapEx will be notably larger than in 2023</td></tr></table>

Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research

3. Investor positioning still light
![](images/d20536b1ed8c1ceb651aee4728cd1f080f5bca2afb0a509cfb3fcb860940f656.jpg)
Source: BSE, RIMES, MSCI, Morgan Stanley Research

...although near-term triggers are missing and relative valuation comfort has come down: Global IT services company Accenture recently indicated further tightening of discretionary spending, which came as a negative surprise to us. We believe the company's commentary is relevant for its India IT peers as well. The key takeaway for India IT services vendors in our view is that: a) deal mix is likely to be skewed towards longer tenor deals, implying slower conversion to revenues; and b) run-offs of past discretionary projects (projects getting completed and not getting renewed) would ensure leakages will remain a drag on growth in the near term. We have therefore lowered our revenue growth forecasts for F25/26 across our coverage universe, driving EPS estimates down. Further, post a sharp fall in Accenture stock, the relative valuation of TCS is now at a premium of 4% to Accenture vs long-term average of 3% - which has closed the arbitrage on multiples.

Exhibit 10: Top 4 India IT Services stocks' P/E premium to Sensex (1Yr Fwd P/E): During the last upcycle, the premium exceeded 1 standard deviation above the mean – now it is slightly lower than +1SD above mean
![](images/7b6f4a7330b19940d1669e11671fa3d5ad5ca9db9c4d78e680e1cb024f8cefab.jpg)
Source: Refinitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

Exhibit 11: TCS premium to ACN: 2-year forward PE: Relative valuation has some room to catch up (%)
![](images/a1621bd1cdb5b7907f7ce4a933f02193bdb6e9dad5d0b8ca0cc59e7c4fff1304.jpg)
Source: Refinitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

How to be positioned in the sector at this juncture? We prefer large caps with stronger order book vs mid caps: At the sector level, we would recommend being positioned as neutral with a positive bias as we cannot rule out a possible bounce-back in 2H after a long period of slow growth. However, within the sector, we would be selective on owning stocks given the possibility of divergence in performance, similar to 2023. We note that a number of stocks in this space have underperformed the Sensex over the last 30 days - even before Accenture reported its results.

Exhibit 12: IT Services stocks (on average) underperformed Sensex (last one month)
![](images/7b697ab936a107d54773a6f4df4d8779563678428f0a19260cc5a0a259b1bb98.jpg)
Source: Past performance is no guarantee of future results. Results shown do not include transaction costs. Source: Refinitiv, Morgan Stanley Research; As of 25th March 2024

Positive biases for large caps with strong order book...: Given limited visibility of timing and the amount of bounce-back from short-cycle project work, we stick with stocks that provide higher revenue growth visibility into FY25 relative to others. In this context, we have a greater preference for large caps (lower bar for expectations) vs mid caps (high ask rate to achieve estimates). Within large caps, Infosys is our preferred name followed by TCS. We upgrade HCLT to OW (from EW) due to its relatively strong visibility into FY25 revenue growth (backed by deal wins growing at 7-8% YoY in FY24e and strong exit rate for services business of 4%) as well as its higher room for margin improvement in the medium term (based on emp + sub con costs as a % of revenues). We understand the starting point valuation for HCLT is not particularly favorable; however, we see no reason for de-rating as we expect HCLT to outperform Infosys on revenue growth for a second year in a row.

Exhibit 13: TMT order booking growth YoY comparison (FY24e vs FY23): Order booking shows stronger comfort for Infosys/TCS relative to other names
![](images/67f8aada524943e4725ccc46499d2cfe7e1f72ae02056f0212160523dea7b28b.jpg)
Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research. For Infosys, the TCV order book is for large deals above USD50mn including renewals. For TCS, Wipro, LTIM order booking is for the entire business including renewals For HCLT, order booking is for new deal wins without including renewals, and for Tech M order booking is for new deal wins above a certain threshold.

Source: Refinitiv, Morgan Stanley Research estimates

...and negative for stocks with poor revenue growth visibility: We see negative biases for stocks like Wipro (overleveraged to short cycle work), Tech M (weak growth visibility with largest vertical seeing material headwinds in demand and risk to consensus margin estimates) and LTIM (possibility of lagging revenue growth vs larger peers like TCS, implying room for valuation discount to widen).

Within mid caps, we prefer Cyient and Coforge: Our preference for Cyient is driven by our expectation of ongoing strong growth in the aerospace industry (supporting favorable growth outlook for vendors like Cyient) and company-specific factors around costs/ margins supporting our view of margin expansion driving EPS growth > revenue growth. We like Coforge as a market share gain story given its strong execution in the respective verticals in which it is present. Even despite a challenging revenue growth outlook, we see Coforge delivering double-digit organic revenue growth in F25. We are UW on Tata Elxsi and LTTS on valuations with room for downside risks to F25e revenue growth forecasts. We are EW on MphasiS but believe it may be heading towards its last leg of consensus EPS cuts (as potential rate cuts in F25 will support a better outlook for F26e). We believe in the event of further de-rating, MphasiS could offer better entry points and we would wait for such an opportunity before turning more constructive on the name.

Key catalysts: We see annual revenue guidance from Infosys and HCLT being the big events ahead that stocks could react to. We believe the risk of disappointing guidance was partially priced in for both of these names following Accenture's results. We see limited room for disappointment on F25 revenue guidance around INFY/HCLT's results, although conversely we do not expect surprise upside before revenue growth improves (2HFY25e). Instead of timing the recovery, we prefer to gauge optimum entry prices and accumulate in the event of any further price declines potentially around guidance announcement events. Historically, Infosys stock has tended to bottom out during April if the company had recently provided disappointing guidance (vs market expectations), opening up buying opportunities.

## Company-specific Commentary

Infosys: The mid point of Infosys' full year F24 revenue guidance of 1.5%-2% implies a slight decline QoQ in 4Q. We expect Infosys to report revenue decline of -0.5% QoQ in 4Q24, indicating FY24e revenue growth of 1.8% YoY in cc terms. Provisions related to the BPO business had previously had a one-time impact on margins of \~60bps; we therefore believe that the lack of such a headwind in 4Q will have acted as a tailwind. Headwinds offsetting this tailwind, in our view, would be a full three-month impact of wage hikes vs two months in 3Q as well as visa-related costs. We expect net additions to headcount to be muted and utilization to see a slight uptick, supporting gross margins. Overall we see room for slight margin expansion QoQ in 4Q, taking the full-year F24e margin to 20.8% vs guidance of 20-22%.

In terms of deal wins, we expect large deals total contract value to be closer to USD2.5- 3bn in 4Q24 with potential net new deals of 45-50%. This would imply net new deal wins in F24 approaching USD8.5bn vs USD4bn in FY23. We believe this would be a significant source of confidence for revenue growth trends to look better in F25 vs F24 for Infosys. We Infosys to start the year with a revenue guidance range of 3-6% YoY, in which the lower end is slightly better than Infosys' actual performance in F24 (\~1.8%). On margins, we expect guidance to be similar to the previous year's range of 20-22%, the mid point of which implies stable margins with a slight positive bias in F25e.

TCS: We expect constant currency revenue growth of 1.6% QoQ in 4Q and USD revenue growth of 1.9% QoQ. This would translate into YoY growth of 2.7% YoY (vs 1.7% YoY in 3Q) and USD growth of 3.1% YoY. International revenue (consolidated minus India) declined -0.3% QoQ in 3Q and we expect this to improve to 1% QoQ in cc terms on the reversal of furloughs. Further, we expect the company's large telecoms contract in India to have a higher component of equipment-related revenue, boosting India revenue growth to 6% QoQ and consolidated revenue growth to 1.6% QoQ in cc terms. We expect net employee addition trends to remain subdued, indicating potential gross margin improvement. We expect EBIT margins of 25.4% (+40bps QoQ) helped primarily by gross margin improvement led by better utilization, productivity and partially offset by slight rupee appreciation. We expect FX gains to be better QoQ, helping other income and net income to come in at Rs121.4bn (+3.5% QoQ).

On deal wins, we believe TCS will have a strong quarter due to seasonality on a QoQ basis. We expect total deal wins total contract value of USD10bn (+23% QoQ, flat YoY) for the quarter, taking total FY24 deal wins to USD39.5bn (+16% YoY). We expect commentary around discretionary spending to remain subdued; however, on the back of strong deal wins, we expect commentary around slightly strong QoQ growth in 1HFY25 for the core business (excluding the telecoms deal).

HCLT: For HCLT we believe the reversal of furloughs and a full three-month contribution from the Verizon deal (vs 2 months contribution in 3QF24) will be key drivers of growth in the Services business. The company's guidance for the Services business of 5-5.5% YoY would imply 4Q growth of 1.6%-3.5% QoQ in cc terms. We assume growth of 2.8% YoY in cc terms in 4Q in Services business. For the software business, we have assumed a decline of 14-15% $\mathsf { Q o Q } ,$ in line with historical trends. This drives our consolidated business revenue growth estimate of +0.6% QoQ in 4Q. On margins we expect Services margins to be largely stable (negatives would be \~30-40bps of wage hikes, 10-15 bps of rupee appreciation and positives would be improving margins in ER&D services). We expect Software business margins to fall significantly $\mathsf { Q o Q }$ in line with last years' trends. Overall we expect consolidated margins to decline to 18.4% vs 19.8% in 3Q. This would imply fullyear margins of \~18.4% – towards the mid point of the guided range of 18-19% for F24e.

On deal wins. we expect HCLT to end the year with total contract value for new deal wins of USD2-2.1bn, taking full-year growth to 7-8% YoY. With this in the bag and a potential impact in 1Q from the divestiture of the State Street BPO business (0.9% impact), overall revenue growth guidance could be 5-7% YoY for F25 with an EBIT margin outlook of 18- 19%. We expect the company to maintain a healthy OCF to net income conversion ratio of 120% in F25, implying 100% conversion of FCF to net income.

Wipro: We expect IT services revenue growth to be within their guided range (-1.5% to +0.5% QoQ in cc) at -0.3% QoQ cc. We expect EBIT margins in the IT services business to improve further in $4 \mathsf Q$ (to 16.4%, +45bps QoQ ) in line with management commentary (despite full impact of wage hike in 4Q vs only one month impact in 3Q). We expect deal wins to be in a similar range as in the last few quarters (USD3.5-4bn). For 1QF25, we expect IT services revenue growth guidance of -0.5% to +1.5% QoQ in cc terms, implying 0.5% QoQ growth at the mid point, which is a positive number after declining for last few quarters. On margins, we expect Wipro to maintain their commentary of keeping margins within a tight band in $4 \mathrm { Q } .$ We expect momentum in Capco and the consulting business to be important commentary to watch out for to understand the underlying trends in deal conversion to revenue metrics.

Tech M: We expect revenues to decline by -1.5% QoQ given the lack of one-time revenue benefits seen in 3QF24 (of 1.4% QoQ). We expect underlying EBIT margins to improve to 7.6% in $4 \mathsf Q$ and this time we don’t expect any further provisions to impact reported EBIT margins. We expect new deal win total contract value in the range of USD500mn – better than 3Q but still weak comparatively. We expect detailed commentary from management on the earnings call in terms of a strategy update. Investors are largely focusing on Tech M improving its margin trends. We expect medium-term commentary and the qualitative outlook to be constructive; however, progress could be gradual as the company will have to make the required investments up front. This could lead to disappointment in F25 margin assumptions for consensus and could drive material F25 EPS cuts from consensus, in our view.

LTIM: We expect LTIM to deliver constant currency revenue growth of -1% QoQ, and similar reported growth with limited cross-currency effects. F3Q seasonally sees the benefit of pass-through revenue, the impact of which is typically 1-1.5% QoQ growth, by our estimates, but management said 3Q saw more than seasonal pass-through revenues, which will likely taper off in $4 \mathrm { Q } ,$ thereby impacting growth in $ 4 \mathrm { Q } .$ Moreover, we expected furloughs seen in $3 0$ to reverse in a staggered fashion through $4 \mathrm { Q } .$ We believe margins could moderate by \~70bps $\mathsf { Q o Q }$ owing to large deal ramp-ups and investments in the business. We expect deal win TCV to remain healthy in ${ \mathsf { F } } 4 { \mathsf { Q } } ,$ even though \~20% growth in 9MF24e may not be easy to sustain. Key to watch will be commentary on the outlook for F25 and any disclosures around service lines giving any indication of revenue synergies coming through and margin levers going forward.

MphasiS: We expect MphasiS to report 2.3% revenue growth in sequential terms, driven by the reversal of most furloughs, as indicated by management in the last conference call, and a ramp-up of deals already won in 9MF24. We expect marginal growth in digital risk and stability in the DXC portion of the business this quarter. Margins could moderate sequentially following the one-off benefits seen last quarter and also due to investments made in the business. Deal wins have been modest in F2Q-3Q, after a strong F1Q, and we do not expect any material pick-up from F3Q levels of US\$240mn this quarter.

LTTS: We expect LTTS to deliver 5.4% QoQ revenue growth in constant currency terms, with a 30bps tailwind from cross-currency in the quarter, leading to reported growth of 5.7%, driven by seasonality in the SWC business. Management stated in their last conference call that full-year revenue guidance implies growth of 4-7% QoQ in 4Q, hence we expect revenue to land towards the mid point of this range. We think deal wins could be strong in 4Q, given that the company has already announced a US\$100mn deal with the Maharashtra State Cyber Department. We expect sequential improvement in margins of \~28bp, driven by higher revenue growth driving operating leverage and other operational efficiencies. For F25, we expect LTTS to guide double-digit growth of 10-12% in constant currency terms.

Tata Elxsi: We expect Tata Elxsi to report 2.7% revenue growth in constant currency terms, boosted by a cross-currency tailwind of 40bps, and resulting in 3.1% revenue growth in reported dollar terms. We expect growth to be driven by the medical devices segment within the Engineering product division, as the media business remains on soft footing and the transportation business is working through challenges on the client side (Tier-1 auto suppliers facing stiff competition from Chinese players) with deals won from OEMs not ramping up on time. We expect margins to improve by \~20bps QoQ as operating leverage from revenue growth is partly offset by continued investments in the business.

Cyient: At the group level, we expect revenue growth of 1.8% QoQ in constant-currency terms. Within the DET business (1.2% QoQ constant currency growth), we expect growth to be driven by aerospace and sustainability verticals. We believe communications could return to growth while new growth areas could remain weak. We are baking in 5% QoQ revenue growth in the DLM business. Margins could moderate slightly QoQ in the DET business on the back of lower utilization and investments in sales and marketing initiatives. On guidance for F25, we expect management to guide for high single digit growth at the low end and double-digit growth at the top end (8-10%) for the DET business with margin guided to expand by up to 50bps YoY.

Coforge: We expect Coforge to deliver revenue growth of 1.9% QoQ in constant currency and reported terms given the limited cross-currency effects at play. We see margins expanding by \~140bps largely in the range of management guidance of QoQ margin improvement in the quarter, supported by the reversal of furlough-related impacts on margins in 3Q (40-50bps). With respect to its 12-month executable order book, Coforge has been adding US\$30-40mn QoQ for the last few quarters, and we believe any acceleration in the said run-rate would provide comfort on the guidance for F25. For F25, we expect Coforge to guide for double-digit revenue growth of 10-13% in constant currency terms.

## Changes to earnings, scenario values

## Discussion on PT Changes

Probability weightings: There are no changes to our probability weightings for stocks under coverage.

Changes to estimates: For most companies in our coverage universe, we moderate our revenue growth assumptions, driven by continued pressure on discretionary spending, which leads to moderation in the pace of recovery of our growth estimates. Accordingly, margin improvement potential also gets pushed out and this leads to trimming of EPS estimates in most cases, partly offset by better currency rates due to INR depreciation.

Changes to price targets: We cut our price targets for stocks by up to 16%, driven by changes to EPS estimates in the near term and some moderation in our longer-term growth assumptions.

## Risk Reward - Tata Consultancy<sub>Risk</sub> <sub>Reward</sub> <sub>–</sub> <sub>Tata</sub> <sub>Consultancy</sub> <sub>Services</sub> <sub>(TCS.NS)</sub>

Good order book and margin improvement potential

## Rs4,350.00PRICE TARGET

DCF, probability-weighted: bull case (20%), base case (60%) and bear case (20%). Our bull case weighting re ects the possibility of a positive surprise from revenue growth, while our bear case weighting re <sup>fl</sup>ects the possibility of a slowdown in the demand environment owing to emerging macro headwinds. We assume a cost of equity of 10.6% and terminal growth rate of 4.5% across scenarios.

Consensus Price Target Distribution

Source: Re finitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

![](images/70f79077815a3a0dd3eef218618bbd408627b0af9b2bc0b12c9b70d2851ddb28.jpg)

RISK REWARD CHART
![](images/129740f82eebace1e07f5b4c13bd90ff2aae626aa620ce3fcc9a8f86bdb020d8.jpg)
Key: Historical Stock Performance Current Stock Price Price Target
Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Lead indicators such as headcount additions have been soft and could remain so in the next two quarters. However, deal wins have been strong, driving better visibility on revenue growth relative to some peers. Based on deal wins, BFSI growth could turn positive for the next 1-2 quarters. ▪ TCS appears well-placed for margin improvement in F25 vs peers. We bake in 50bps yoy improvement in F25. ▪ The stock underperformed peers in 2023. P/E multiples are not cheap but premium could be sustained if execution improves.

![](images/eee88782226a5ba9aaf0e9f543b06cee06e1599e7095cb7a403c099e7db0e781.jpg)

## Risk Reward Themes

Pricing Power: Positive View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

## Rs5,500.00

## Implies 32.5x bull case on F26e EPS

Acceleration despite macro challenges: Continued acceleration in tech spending and strong execution help TCS continue to grow ahead of most peers in the near to medium term.

## BASE CASE

## Rs4,200.00

## Implies \~27.3x base case on F26e EPS

Gradual recovery to aid revenue growth: We expect revenue growth to gradually recover through the quarters ahead as some macro challenges abate. TCS is well positioned to participate and along with revenue growth delivers on margin expansion as well.

## BEAR CASE

## Rs3,650.00

Implies \~25.2x bear-case on F26e EPS Uncertain macro climate: TCS adds lower incremental USD revenues in F24 and F25; hence, growth rates remain subdued.

![](images/f35f76cb390bf6c822494cf244702057562a7e7d51d3c9453a77553496e4f33b.jpg)

## RISKS TO DOWNSIDE

Escalations in geopolitical environment and in ation risks hampering the global macro <sup>fl</sup>environment

Rising attrition trends across the industry

## Risk Reward - Infosys Lim<sub>Risk Reward – Infosys Limited (INFY.NS)</sub>

Expect healthy recovery in F25 with strong deal wins

## Rs1,750.00PRICE TARGET

DCF, probability-weighted: bull case (20%), bear case (20%), base case (60%). The bull case weighting re ects the possibility of a positive surprise from revenue growth, while the bear case weighting re <sup>fl</sup>ects the possibility of a slowdown in the demand environment owing to emerging macro headwinds. We assume a 10.8% cost of equity and 4.5% terminal growth across scenarios.

Consensus Price Target Distribution

Source: Re finitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

## RISK REWARD CHART

![](images/40540e76b1321f88ae184615a819245a3d862da4bcbf29578e989a5a15ccee31.jpg)

Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ We view Infosys as a high-quality large-cap stock with good client diversi cation and a strong customer base. In F24, however, we believe it could lag TCS slightly on revenue growth.

▪ We think attrition levels have normalized. Changing deal contours and normalization of other costs could continue to affect margins in $F 2 4 ,$ but they could recover in F25.

▪ We expect recovery in F25 driven by strong deal wins; as a result, the drag on P/E multiples from guidance cuts could narrow, providing upside potential for Infosys

## Risk Reward Themes

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ HCLT has executed well in the Services business, with strong growth in F22-24e driven by strong execution on order book. We see better revenue growth visibility for HCLT in F25 as well, driven by strong exit rate and decent deal wins.
▪ Margin levels provide upside potential from here on, even though near-term
triggers could be limited.
▪ We understand starting point of valuation for HCLT is not good; however, we see no reason for de-rating given our expectations for HCLT outperforming Infosys on revenue growth for a second year in a row.

## UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Wipro's revenue growth has lagged peers for the past few quarters, and we believe this could continue given the exit rate for FY24 will be low despite exposure to Consulting and short cycle deals (which are showing initial signs of turning) as we expect industry recovery to be gradual unlike prior cycles.

▪ Margins have scope to improve, but recovery should be gradual there as well. ▪ Wipro's P/E multiple discount to Infy will likely narrow post the gap up, but post that there should be limited room for any outperformance.

## Rs1,190.00PRICE TARGET

Our bull case probability re ects potential improvement in client spending intentions as Tech M capitalizes on its proven strength in the Communications vertical. ## UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ The near-term revenue growth outlook remains murky given client-speci c challenges and its highest exposure to the Telecom vertical. We see Tech M's revenue growth recovering with a lag vs. peers. ▪ With management changes, buy-side expectations skewed toward accelerated margin recovery appear a tall task to us, and limit the likelihood of a potential positive surprise.

▪ The stock is trading at a signi cant premium to its historical two-year forward P/E. We expect consensus EPS estimates to be reset meaningfully for F24/25, which should drive underperformance.

## Rs7,300.00PRICE TARGET

DCF, probability-weighted: bull case (20%), bear case (20%), base case (60%). The bull-case weighting re ects the possibility of a positive surprise from revenue growth, while the bearcase weighting re <sup>fl</sup>ects the possibility of a slowdown in the demand environment owing to emerging macro headwinds. Cost of equity of 11.9% and terminal growth of 5% across scenarios.

## Consensus Price Target Distribution

## UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Tata Elxsi is a niche ER&D Service provider with a presence across the high-growth
areas of Automotive, Media and Medical Devices. Its capabilities are well-rounded. However, in the current macroenvironment, demand recovery continues to be delayed. ▪ Margins have been resilient and, despite investments in the business, continue to trend in a similar range.
▪ Stock has underperformed its mid-cap
peers in the last year, but with delay in
growth recovery, high consensus
expectations and premium valuations, we see limited upside catalysts.

![](images/85682de4f2999ab9cf25ca00bff3ce427636169fb961d18d50c3b2479fcde55b.jpg)

## Risk Reward Themes

Disruption: Ne<sub>g</sub>ative Pricing Power: Positive

## BULL CASE

## Rs10,800.00

## 55.7x F2026e bull-case EPS

Revenue growth momentum accelerates with stable margins: Tata Elxsi accelerates strong growth trends by increasing the contribution of IP revenues in the pro le and client mining in top 25 accounts. It is able to increase margins at current levels, as offshoring does not reverse and high-margin IP revenues support its margin pro le.

## BASE CASE

## Rs6,900.00

## \~39.1x F2026e base-case EPS

Good revenue growth momentum with resilient margins: We expect stronger than peers' growth trends to continue, driven by new client additions and client mining in its top 25 accounts, and see margins being sustained.

## BEAR CASE

## Rs5,000.00

## 30.8x F2026e bear-case EPS

Moderate revenue growth momentum with moderating margins: Tata Elxsi's revenue growth rate moderates as a result of either business cyclicality because diversi cation is low, client-speci c issues at top clients or inability to push further for IPs. Margins moderate over the medium term, driven by investments, reversal of offshoring and movement to low-margin adjacencies.

## Rs4,800.00PRICE TARGET

Bull case weighting re ects a possible positive surprise from revenue growth once the demand environment improves, while the bear case weighting re <sup>fl</sup>ects the chance of a further slowdown in the demand environment owing to continued macro headwinds. Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ LTIM appears well positioned to participate in the IT Services growth opportunity, driven by its large portfolio of services, steady outlook for top clients, and potential merger-related revenue synergies such as increased participation in large deals and mining existing clients more deeply. ▪ Margins have room to expand from current levels, but given the reset of expectations by management improvement could be gradual. ▪ The stock has underperformed on a YTD basis, due to which relative valuation premium has come off vs larger peers, which protects the downside in our view.

![](images/08e586e36f976e0b48fe9abcbf0afc099142d40f8f6469be489079d3d9cb9804.jpg)

## UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ LTTS is India's largest pure-play ER&D Service provider. It has rich domain experience from its L&T parentage and signi <sup>fi</sup>cant client mining potential. The company's business model is well diversi ed to protect revenues from the vagaries of business cycles, but its revenue growth pro file has been moderate as a result in an underpenetrated and fast growing market, which we view negatively. ▪ We project a revenue CAGR of \~14% and EPS CAGR of \~17% over F24-26 with improvement in margins, which would be moderate relative to its most ER&D peers. ▪ Two-year forward P/E is above its historical average (since 2016) and at a premium to IT peers such as LTIM, making risk-reward unfavorable, in our view.

![](images/3c6ec438b4bfcdc3af02ef36021548d0217e2136df888be613407290d982c93f.jpg)

Rs4,200.00

## BEAR CASE

Rs3,500.00

## 22.4x F26e bear-case EPS

Slow growth momentum and declining margins: 1) LTTS faces client-speci c issues (as in F20) and vertical-speci c issues (as during Covid-19) that derail its growth trajectory. 2) Its margin pro le declines from current levels as revenue growth moderates.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ Pipeline growth remains strong and, as client-speci c issues ebb over time, MphasiS can start delivering sequential revenue growth. MphasiS is also a rate cut play in the sense that it can see an upturn in the Digital Risk business with increasing volumes in the mortgage business, even though such bene fit may flow in the second leg of rate cuts after clients fill their internal capacities, in our view.

▪ The recent acquisition of Silverline has brought another reset of margin levels, similar to the reset after the Blink acquisition in 2021. We are baking in EBIT margins of 14.8% in F25 and 15.2% in F26. ▪ The valuations are inexpensive which makes risk-reward balanced, in our view.

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Revenue growth is becoming broad-based with aerospace and sustainability leading the show, and the connectivity vertical bottoming out.

▪ The company has surprised positively on Services business gross margins; incremental margin levers will be revenue growth and offshoring.

▪ Valuation (P/E) in absolute terms looks expensive compared to its historical trading range, but, in our view, on a relative basis, valuation is reasonable vs. peers, especially in light of consensus F25e EPS holding up for Cyient while moderating for some of the ER&D peers.

## Risk Reward - Coforge L<sub>Risk Reward – Coforge Ltd (COFO.NS)</sub>

Demonstrating Scalability Attributes

## Rs6,500.00PRICE TARGET

We use a probability-weighted average of risk-reward scenario values, derived from DCF, to arrive at our price target. We use a cost of equity of 12.3% and a terminal growth rate of 5.5% across scenarios. The bull case (20% weight) re <sup>fl</sup>ects the possibility of a positive revenue growth surprise once the demand environment improves, while the bear case (20%) re ects the possibility of a further slowdown in demand owing to macro headwinds.

## RISK REWARD CHART

![](images/805fa991f3eb1a62a4e3b3ef61875cc25be3d585b9a40f6c4d65ff9101845a07.jpg)
Key: Historical Stock Performance Current Stock Price Price Target
Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

We think Coforge has a sustainable revenue growth pro le, driven by deep domain
experience, its transition to larger clients, and client-mining success with increasing size and velocity of deals. We think Coforge can grow higher for longer vs. its mid-cap peers under our coverage.
Coforge's gross margin has room to expand, driven by the normalisation of costs and an uptick in offshoring levels and new graduate deployment.
Valuations are at a premium but have the potential to sustain driven by: a) strong FCF conversion; b) stable payout ratios; and c) strong revenue growth pro le.

## Consensus Rating Distribution

![](images/1db835e90a8c9556df1ddc9220a1911c2e822a907ba9a2d328aebda2f413801a.jpg)

## Risk Reward Themes

Self-help: Positive View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

## Rs8,700.00

## Revenue growth accelerates with strong margins

We assume: 1) Coforge accelerates the strong growth trends driven by pent-up demand for services business and deep mining in its top client buckets. 2) Coforge is able to expand margins signi cantly as strong revenue growth provides operating leverage, offshoring continues to see an uptick, the employee pyramid improves and some of the drag from portfolio-speci c issues such as the India business and AdvantageGo abate.

## BASE CASE

## Rs6,600.00

## Strong revenue growth with improving margins

We expect: 1) Coforge continues to deliver strong growth trends driven by client mining in its top 32 accounts, newer partnerships entered with OEMs and scaleup of emerging verticals. 2) Margins increase over the medium term driven by gross margin improvement from cost normalization, further uptick in offshoring and higher new graduate onboarding.

## BEAR CASE

## Rs4,000.00

## Moderate revenue growth with stable margins

We assume: 1) revenue growth moderates either due to macro surprising on the downside or vertical-speci c and/or clientspeci c issues. 2) Margins are stable over the medium term driven by investments, as planned improvements from offshoring and new graduate deployment are hard to come by.

Deal win TCV and executable order book

Rate of new logo (i.e., client) additions

Business mix improvement helping realizations, thereby driving better margins

Free cash ow conversion and dividend payouts

Management stability and employee attrition

## Analyst Stock Ratings

Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.

## Analyst Industry Views

Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.
